# Tasks: Secret Cleanup Remediation and Verification

**Input**: [spec.md](spec.md), [plan.md](plan.md), [research.md](research.md), [data-model.md](data-model.md), [contract](contracts/cleanup-and-evidence.md), [quickstart](quickstart.md).
**Branch**: `codex/065-security-secret-cleanup` | **Generated**: 2026-11-13
**Tests**: Explicitly required by FR-010 through FR-014 and the four stories. Write meaningful failing behavior checks before each source correction, then run/repair/rerun that group before closure. No runtime execution is claimed by this list.
**Organization**: Setup, shared foundation, US1/US2/US3 (P1), US4 (P2), cross-cutting closure. Paths are repository-relative. Existing modules/source sets and toolchain only.

## Format and execution rules

Every task has an unchecked checkbox, sequential ID and file path. Story tasks carry their US label. `[P]` means disjoint files and no mutual dependency **within the stated dependency wave**, not permission to bypass prerequisites. Tasks sharing an evidence report are serialized. A task that requires unavailable hardware/review remains unchecked with an Unverified record; execution failure is Failed, and Deferred requires a specific authorized deferral. No changing gates, skipped tests or fabricated evidence to finish the list.

**Purpose**: Establish reproducible scope and safe evidence handling without changing product behavior.

## Phase 3: US1 — End a sensitive interaction safely (P1, MVP)

**Goal**: Erase owned plaintext and release permitted references when vault/passkey interactions terminate, without harming active borrowers.
**Independent test**: Synthetic vault edit/save/cancel and passkey success/rejection/cancellation/session teardown demonstrate owned-buffer erasure, borrower preservation and post-terminal runtime retention findings. Clipboard scheduling is independently delivered by US2; diagnostic outputs by US3.

## Phase 4: US2 — Copy a password with truthful cleanup behavior (P1)

**Goal**: Fixed30 best-effort scheduling, prepublication sensitive marking, non-content ownership checks, safe skips and honest result feedback.
**Independent test**: Virtual-time/mocked boundary assertions plus synthetic physical cases distinguish correct scheduling/attempt/skip/failure from observed removal, including background/interruption and replacement limitations.

### Tests before implementation

## Dependencies and execution order

```text
T001 -> T002 -> T003 -> T004 -> T005 -> T006 -> T007
Foundation -> US1 tests -> US1 fixes/heap/checkpoint
Foundation -> US2 tests -> T019 shared contract -> T020 -> T021 -> caller integration -> US2 physical/checkpoint
Foundation -> US3 matrix/tests -> source fixes -> configured output/checkpoint
US1 + US2 + US3 -> US4 candidate reconciliation -> independent review
T039/T043 policy and register synchronization + preliminary US4 reconciliation -> freeze product candidate -> final T036 reconciliation against frozen commit/tree -> T041 final regressions -> refreshed T037 validation -> T042 independent review -> T044/T045 evidence disposition/retention
```

- Default execution is sequential in ID order except that T043 precedes candidate freeze, final T036 reconciliation, T041 final regressions, refreshed T037 validation and T042 review; T036 may begin earlier but is completed only after freeze. US1 P1 is the first demonstrable increment; US2 and US3 are also P1, and US4 is P2.
- T008/T009/T010 can be authored in parallel after the foundation; they touch different suites. Their fixes remain serialized where owners overlap. T015/T016 require the actual relevant corrections and synthetic setup.
- T017 then T018 may be authored concurrently after the foundation; T019 changes their API expectations. Keep T019–T025 an integrated compile-safe migration, not independently shippable incompatible signatures. T024 depends on T019 and its finding assessment; T025 requires all relevant callers. T026–T028 follow adapter/caller completion.
- T030 and T031 are independent after T029. T032 follows both; T033 must follow US2's T022 for the shared iOS file. US3 output assertions may begin with injected synthetic paths without waiting for full US1/US2, but final destination evidence must match the integrated candidate.
- US4's format review can be prepared early, but T036–T042 closure depends on US1–US3 current-candidate results; independent review follows the completed required evidence package. T039 and T043 must complete before freezing the product commit/tree; final T036 reconciliation must then match that frozen identity before final T037 validation. T041 final regressions and refreshed T037 validation must pass before T042 independent review. Keep later review/disposition evidence separate and record any delivery revision comparison. Any later change to reviewed material or product/build requires a new frozen candidate, impact assessment, refreshed affected evidence and renewed independent review before T044 closes anything. Evidence/disposition-only additions must not change reviewed product or policy scope.
- Device/heap/diagnostic capture procedures are serialized even when source/test authoring is parallel. This list identifies opportunities, not an instruction to spawn agents or run competing device workloads.

## Parallel examples per story

| Story | Safe authoring wave | Integration boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| US1 | T008 vault tests, T009 FIDO ownership tests, T010 seed-copy tests after T007 | Owner corrections and shared evidence writes serialized; heap after fixes. |
| US2 | T017 service tests and T018 vault tests after T007 | One shared API/adapter/caller migration; no parallel edits of the Android service. |
| US3 | T030 writer-output tests and T031 crypto emission tests after T029 | Writer/source corrections after tests; iOS logging after T022; T046 follows T029 and covers newly discovered Bluetooth identifier/exception emissions. |
| US4 | Candidate inventory comparison for T036 and read-only artifact preflight for T037 can be prepared independently | Complete final T036 against the frozen candidate after T039/T043 before validating the final index; record/review writes and acceptance serialized. |

## Requirement coverage and implementation strategy

| Requirements | Primary tasks |
| --- | --- |
| FR-001/002/004 | T001, T004–T007, T009/T012, T024, T036/T039 |
| FR-003/005/010 | T006, T008–T016, T018–T025, T068–T069 |
| FR-006/007/012 | T002, T017–T028 |
| FR-008/009 | T007, T010/T013/T024, T029–T035, T046, T059, T061–T067 |
| FR-011/014/015 | T002/T003, T015/T016, T026/T027/T034, T038/T045 |
| FR-013/016 | T001/T003, all retained story results, T036–T045 |

**MVP**: Foundation plus US1's synthetic vault/passkey terminal-cleanup increment, with its own focused and runtime checkpoint. It is an ownership improvement, not full release-audit acceptance. Add the integrated US2 clipboard increment, then US3 diagnostics, then US4's candidate package and independent review. Do not mark lower-level work verified from task authoring or skip an unavailable mandatory runtime/review task.

Assessment is intentionally evidence-led: T004–T007 freeze a finite source/finding set; any additional discovered defect receives a concrete path/test/remediation task before its owner can close. This avoids pretending a pre-audit list already enumerates every source defect. No new production module, dependency, settings flow, alarm/service or constitutional exemption is authorized here.
